===== agent-docs/FINAL_CHANGE_SPECIFICATION_RPR-0.6.md (1-162) =====
   1: # Replay / Production Business-Logic Parity
   2: 
   3: **Revision:** RPR-0.6-DRAFT  
   4: **Status:** NOT APPROVED ƒ?" IMPLEMENTATION FORBIDDEN  
   5: **Baseline:** `0cb6a9a6cc64ba8ac9d33797bcc30af534fd46e3`; the complete dirty-worktree snapshot must be re-hashed and approved immediately before implementation.  
   6: **Supersedes:** RPR-0.4/RPR-0.5 for review only.
   7: 
   8: ## 1. Business objective and flow
   9: 
  10: Replay must use current Production paper-trading policies with an isolated
  11: virtual account. It must never read Production account state, call HTTP, or
  12: call candle-only output historical parity.
  13: 
  14: 1. User selects one symbol, virtual profile, window and mode.
  15: 2. Qualifier validates every input before a generated Replay artifact exists.
  16: 3. A refusal writes only a run/certificate diagnostic.
  17: 4. Qualified analysis uses closed, identity-matching candles.
  18: 5. Simulation invokes pure Production policies with only virtual adapters.
  19: 6. Results stage privately and publish only on terminal success.
  20: 
  21: Release 1 labels outcomes only `ANALYSIS_PARITY`,
  22: `PRODUCTION_LOGIC_SIMULATION`, or `REFUSED_INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE`.
  23: `ENTRY_DECISION_PARITY` and historical wallet-lifecycle parity are out of scope:
  24: they require account-state evidence that Replay is forbidden to read.
  25: 
  26: ## 2. Root cause, evidence and scope
  27: 
  28: Replay currently can include open candles, has null IDs, reads
  29: `wallet_settings`, and differs from Production price/signal management.
  30: Evidence is current source in `RegressionTestWorker`,
  31: `ShadowProductionReplayService`, `ExecutionReplayScope`,
  32: `TradeExecutionValidationService`, `DynamicProfitLockService`,
  33: `LivePositionProtectionService`, `PositionManagementService`, and
  34: `PaperTradingService`.
  35: 
  36: Close-message lineage, deployed JAR/config identity and historical
  37: order-book/derivative/sentiment availability are not durable for all old
  38: windows. Strict claims are therefore unreachable for such eras unless a future
  39: approved forward-capture design is implemented.
  40: 
  41: In scope: single-symbol virtual simulation; qualifier/refusal; closed-candle
  42: identity; virtual settings/constraints; stable Replay IDs; pure policy ports;
  43: staging; resource limits; tests. Out: Production strategy change, real exchange,
  44: Production account reads, multi-symbol scheduling, and retirement of
  45: FIX-11K/FIX-11Q/pre-FIX122/EXPERIMENTAL. Those paths remain explicit
  46: `EXPERIMENTAL` and cannot enter baseline output.
  47: 
  48: ## 3. Read/write boundary
  49: 
  50: | Class | Data |
  51: |---|---|
  52: | Forbidden Production account state | `wallet_asset`, `wallet_trade`, `wallet_managed_position`, `paper_position`, `wallet_settings`, account snapshots and daily account statistics. |
  53: | Permitted read-only evidence | `candle`, `trade_signal`, `market_price_event`, `signal_processing_work`, `candle_analysis_outcome`, `order_book_snapshot`, `fix122_evaluation`, `buy_htf_assessment_audit`, `execution_opportunity`, subject to qualifier as-of rules. |
  54: | Replay-owned writes | `regression_test_run`, `analysis_test_signal`, `wallet_execution_test`, `wallet_position_test`, and new RPR6 profile/certificate/staging objects. |
  55: 
  56: `NON_CANONICAL` and `PROTECTION_NOT_APPLIED` observations are deliberate
  57: exclusions. Required accepted observations are `LEGACY`/`APPLIED`, retaining id
  58: and ordering slot.
  59: 
  60: ## 4. Complete class/file registry
  61: 
  62: | ID | Artifact | Change | Production impact |
  63: |---|---|---|---|
  64: | RPR6-01 | `RegressionTestWorker` | qualifier before staging; closed identity timeline | none |
  65: | RPR6-02 | `ReplayEvidenceQualifier` new | certificate/mode/refusal | none |
  66: | RPR6-03 | profile/store new | immutable virtual account/config | none |
  67: | RPR6-04 | virtual-account adapter new | balances/positions/effects | none |
  68: | RPR6-05 | `ExecutionReplayScope` | stable run keys, clocks, finally cleanup | shared; no non-Replay behavior change |
  69: | RPR6-06 | `ShadowProductionReplayService` | staging, truthful labels, ordered calls | none |
  70: | RPR6-07 | `TradeExecutionValidationService` | Replay settings port | shared; Production regression/latency proof |
  71: | RPR6-08 | `DynamicProfitLockService` | Replay settings port | shared; Production regression/latency proof |
  72: | RPR6-09 | `LivePositionProtectionService`, `PositionManagementService`, `PaperTradingService` | expose pure ordered inputs, no policy reorder | shared; transaction trace/regression |
  73: | RPR6-10 | `PositionExitPolicy`, `ProfitLockPolicy`, `BinanceMinimumExecutionPolicy` | virtual constraints, no Replay HTTP | shared; existing Production provider unchanged |
  74: | RPR6-11 | `OneCandleContinuationGraceReplayObserver` | experimental profile port and label | Replay only |
  75: | RPR6-12 | `RegimeStateService` | guaranteed Replay cleanup | shared; no Production behavior change |
  76: | RPR6-13 | `ReplayParityValidator` | analysis-only oracle | Replay only |
  77: | RPR6-14 | next verified Flyway migration | profile/certificate/staging/trace schema | additive only |
  78: | RPR6-15 | typed replay configuration | explicit modes and resource bounds | runtime protection |
  79: | RPR6-16 | tests | A8 suite | none |
  80: | RPR6-17 | docs | A10 final docs | none |
  81: 
  82: ## 5. Method contract
  83: 
  84: | ID | Area | Required behavior |
  85: |---|---|---|
  86: | M01 | worker start | qualifier replaces the later pending-evidence write guard; it runs before any Replay business write. |
  87: | M02 | timeline/snapshot | require `closed=1` and exact symbol/TF/open/close identity; mismatch refuses before staging. |
  88: | M03 | context | select stored as-of evidence, historical recomputation, or refusal per dependency; never fallback to future data. |
  89: | M04 | staging | no skip semantics. Any mid-run invariant/error discards staged artifacts and records only terminal run failure. |
  90: | M05 | profile port | wallet/account/HTTP access in Replay throws named forbidden-dependency failure before publication. |
  91: | M06 | order | price then signal path: advisory stop, TP without extension, label-free lock, rebase/floor, SELL/add suppression, progressive add. Simulation records price assumptions. |
  92: | M07 | virtual budget | virtual daily-check exception ends `FAILED_PROFILE_EVALUATION`; never consults Production account data. |
  93: | M08 | scope | clear Replay and `RegimeStateService` state in `finally` for success, cancel and error. |
  94: 
  95: ## 6. Database/configuration/logging
  96: 
  97: | ID | Item | Requirement |
  98: |---|---|---|
  99: | D01 | migration | allocate next free migration only after read-only Flyway-history preflight; never edit V103/V106ƒ?"V109. |
 100: | D02 | MySQL validation | prove baseline/upgrade; V103 collector dependency must be explicit, never silently satisfied. |
 101: | C01 | `replay.default-mode` | `PRODUCTION_LOGIC_SIMULATION`. |
 102: | C02 | `replay.max-concurrent-runs` | `1`. |
 103: | C03 | `replay.profile.required` | `true`; no wallet-settings fallback. |
 104: | C04 | executor/pool limit | bounded; reject before harming Production freshness. |
 105: | L01 | INFO `REPLAY_QUALIFIED` | run/mode/baseline/config/evidence counts. |
 106: | L02 | INFO `REPLAY_REFUSED` | run/requested mode/reason codes. |
 107: | L03 | DEBUG `REPLAY_DECISION` | run/key/route/result/evidence IDs/order/clocks. |
 108: | L04 | WARN `REPLAY_FORBIDDEN_DEPENDENCY` | component/dependency type/run, no secret. |
 109: | L05 | INFO `REPLAY_COMPLETED` | earned mode/rows/duration/resources. |
 110: 
 111: ## 7. Trading behavior
 112: 
 113: Production policy is unchanged. Replayƒ?Ts entry/exit/add/TP/lock/stop behavior
 114: changes only by calling the same extracted pure policies with virtual account
 115: data. Fees/slippage are profile data and zero-fee remains explicitly labelled
 116: when selected. Historical-only/skipped work is reproduced from durable outcome
 117: evidence or analysis parity is refused. Same-boundary higher-timeframe
 118: visibility requires recorded/inferred ordering; otherwise strict analysis claim
 119: is refused.
 120: 
 121: ## 8. Tests and validation
 122: 
 123: | ID | Scenario | Expected result |
 124: |---|---|---|
 125: | X01 | open/identity-mismatched candle | refusal before output |
 126: | X02 | missing context/config/lineage | analysis claim refused; simulation only with declared inputs |
 127: | X03 | excluded price event status | no false evidence failure |
 128: | X04 | account or HTTP port | fail before publication |
 129: | X05 | Replay signal key | stable and never equal to Production ID; near-TP/consumption works |
 130: | X06 | Production policy fixtures | exact ordered stop/TP/lock/floor/SELL/add outcomes |
 131: | X07 | virtual budget exception | named failed run, no account read |
 132: | X08 | experimental observer | baseline never invokes; output labelled experimental |
 133: | X09 | cancellation/error | state cleanup and staged artifact discard |
 134: | X10 | concurrent Replay/load | one-run/pool bounds preserve freshness |
 135: | X11 | real MySQL migration | next migration, B105/V103 policy and upgrade path proven |
 136: | X12 | baseline hash | CI rejects mismatched source/config request |
 137: 
 138: ## 9. Risks and rollback
 139: 
 140: Risks: policy drift through shared extraction, latency, look-ahead, Flyway
 141: collision and false labels. Mitigate with pure ports, Production regression and
 142: transaction tests, qualification refusal, preflight and mode metrics. Rollback
 143: disables the new Replay route; it never falls back to wallet reads. Schema is
 144: additive and corrected only forward.
 145: 
 146: ## 10. Documentation and reconciliation

 151: Implementation report must map every RPR6/M/D/C/L/X item and every actual file
 152: to this registry; any missing or unregistered material work rejects acceptance.

===== codex-docs/TRADING_RULES.md (1-21) =====
   1: # FIX-154 higher-timeframe BUY rule ƒ?" 2026-10-10
   2: 
   3: - `data_quality_valid=true` is required before a bearish 1h signal can be
   4:   treated as trusted. `NULL` is unknown and fails closed for bearish evidence.
   5: - A trusted `STRONG_SELL` at `confidence_score >= 70` blocks the BUY.
   6: - A trusted `SELL`, or trusted `STRONG_SELL` below 70, is a SOFT conflict:
   7:   profiles receive effective 1h `NEUTRAL`, and any allowed initial BUY is
   8:   capped at 25%.
   9: - A bearish 5m still returns `5M_BEARISH_VETO` before any 1h result.
  10: - Existing progressive/add rules retain their raw 1h requirement; a SOFT 1h
  11:   never authorizes an add.
  12: 
  13: # FIX-153 candle continuity rule ƒ?" 2026-10-10
  14: 
  15: - Missing price evidence never becomes invented OHLCV data and never bypasses
  16:   the data-quality gate.
  17: - Automatic reconciliation may restore only one exact, final Binance candle.
  18:   Its recovered/backfilled row has no close token and cannot directly cause
  19:   analysis, a signal, or a trade.
  20: - A later normal live close is the only route that resumes ordinary signal
  21:   generation after a repaired quality window.

===== codex-docs/TRADE_INVESTIGATION_AGENT.md (1-183) =====
   1: # Trade Investigation Agent
   2: 
   3: ## Role
   4: 
   5: Investigate one Crypto AI Trader trade from durable evidence. Explain what
   6: happened, why the system entered and exited, whether it followed the intended
   7: rules, and whether the entry/exit was supported by the information available
   8: at that time.
   9: 
  10: Investigations are read-only. Do not change code, configuration, services,
  11: database records, review states, Replay work, or deployment state.
  12: 
  13: ## Required input
  14: 
  15: Obtain or resolve:
  16: 
  17: - Environment and application: local, test, or production.
  18: - Database host, port, and schema.
  19: - One trade reference: `paper_position.id`, `wallet_trade.id`, or a symbol plus
  20:   a bounded approximate entry-time window.
  21: - Optional question or suspected issue.
  22: 
  23: Before analysis, confirm the symbol, trade/position ID, running version or FIX
  24: level, and ingestion mode. Read the actual schema before choosing tables or
  25: columns. Do not assume a table is in `crypto_ai` when it may belong to
  26: `crypto_ai_v2`.
  27: 
  28: ## Evidence rules
  29: 
  30: - Use configured database and SSH/log connections without displaying secrets.
  31: - Use bounded, read-only queries and targeted log searches.
  32: - Treat stored timestamps as UTC; display both UTC and KSA (`UTC+03:00`).
  33: - Attach record IDs and timestamped log evidence to every factual conclusion.
  34: - Distinguish **confirmed fact**, **supported inference**, and **missing or
  35:   conflicting evidence**.
  36: - Missing logs or records do not prove that no execution/effect occurred.
  37: - A saved signal does not prove execution. A callback return does not prove a
  38:   transaction committed. Under FIX-153, `market_price_event.APPLIED` proves a
  39:   committed successful protection callback, but can be a no-op when there was
  40:   no open position; find the position/trade mutation separately.
  41: - Current configuration does not prove which settings were active at entry. If
  42:   no configuration history exists, report that limitation.
  43: 
  44: ## Investigation workflow
  45: 
  46: ### 1. Resolve identity and environment
  47: 
  48: Link the trade reference to, where available:
  49: 
  50: - `paper_position`
  51: - `wallet_trade`
  52: - entry and exit `trade_signal`
  53: - `signal_processing_work`
  54: - wallet/position execution records
  55: 
  56: Record entry and exit times in UTC and KSA. Confirm whether the trader ran in
  57: local-candle OFF mode or a legacy shared-market mode.
  58: 
  59: ### 2. Collect a small evidence bundle
  60: 
  61: Start with exact IDs and a narrow time window. Expand only when the evidence
  62: requires it. Collect:
  63: 
  64: | Evidence | Establishes |
  65: | --- | --- |
  66: | `paper_position` | lifecycle, entry/exit, stop, target, status |
  67: | `wallet_trade` | executed quantity, prices, fees, linked signal |
  68: | `trade_signal` | decision, strategy, scores, entry eligibility, reasons |
  69: | `signal_processing_work` | claim, attempt, completion, expiry, uncertainty |
  70: | `candle` / `technical_indicator` | market data and analysis inputs available then |
  71: | `market_price_event` | intra-candle protection observations and outcomes |
  72: | configuration/version evidence | applicable rules, when historically available |
  73: | application and timing logs | analysis, execution, protection, exceptions, waits |
  74: 
  75: Avoid fetching large signal explanation fields until they are needed.
  76: 
  77: ### 3. Reconstruct the timeline
  78: 
  79: Build a timeline, explicitly showing missing stages:
  80: 
  81: `candle closed ƒ+' signal saved ƒ+' work claimed ƒ+' locks acquired ƒ+' entry committed ƒ+' protection observations ƒ+' exit decision ƒ+' exit committed`
  82: 
  83: Measure separately:
  84: 
  85: - candle close to signal generation;
  86: - signal generation to processing/entry;
  87: - observation time to protection completion;
  88: - exit-trigger evidence to recorded exit.
  89: 
  90: Correlate delays only with logs/events for the same symbol, position, signal,
  91: or price-event identity. A nearby slow SQL query is not proof of causation.
  92: 
  93: ### 4. Assess entry correctness and quality
  94: 
  95: Use only evidence that was available at entry. Check:
  96: 
  97: - signal mode, closed-candle identity, freshness, and data quality;
  98: - final decision and entry-allowed result;
  99: - BTC context, higher-timeframe, ATR, liquidity, risk/reward, sizing, and
 100:   strategy gates that applied at that time;
 101: - correct signal/position linkage and absence of duplicate/stale-retry entry;
 102: - quantity, wallet balance, risk limits, fees, and fill/reference-price gap.
 103: 
 104: Return two separate conclusions:
 105: 
 106: - **Execution correctness:** followed or violated the implemented rules.
 107: - **Entry quality:** supported, weak, or unverifiable from contemporaneous
 108:   evidence.
 109: 
 110: Subsequent price movement may evaluate the outcome; it must never be presented
 111: as information the entry decision had.
 112: 
 113: ### 5. Reconstruct protection and stop-loss behavior
 114: 
 115: For FIX-153, inspect accepted `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` price events and retain
 116: `PENDING`, `NON_CANONICAL`, and `PROTECTION_NOT_APPLIED` records as evidence
 117: gaps or exclusions. Never substitute candle OHLC for absent intra-candle price
 118: evidence.
 119: 
 120: For each relevant accepted observation:
 121: 
 122: 1. Determine the then-active stop, target, trailing stop, and profit-lock state.
 123: 2. Find the first accepted observation that met an exit threshold.
 124: 3. Confirm that protection evaluated it and identify the resulting position or
 125:    wallet execution record.
 126: 4. Explain delays, rejections, stale/out-of-order classification, exceptions,
 127:    lock waits, or pool pressure.
 128: 
 129: Classify the exit path accurately: fixed stop-loss, trailing stop, profit-lock,
 130: take-profit, signal-driven exit, manual exit, or unknown.
 131: 
 132: ### 6. Assess exit correctness and quality
 133: 
 134: Compare the recorded exit reason, quantity, and price with trigger evidence.
 135: Check threshold reach, accepted-observation identity, protection timing, and
 136: the committed execution. Calculate maximum favourable/adverse movement during
 137: the holding period separately from the contemporaneous exit assessment.
 138: 
 139: ### 7. Reconcile financial results
 140: 
 141: Recalculate using execution records:
 142: 
 143: - bought/sold quantities and weighted entry/exit prices;
 144: - gross P&L, fees, and net P&L;
 145: - partial exits, remaining quantity, and realized versus unrealized values.
 146: 
 147: Flag discrepancies among wallet records, position records, and UI output.
 148: 
 149: ### 8. Diagnose cause and use Replay carefully
 150: 
 151: Classify findings as one or more of:
 152: 
 153: - strategy decision;
 154: - data freshness or coverage;
 155: - analysis delay;
 156: - execution failure;
 157: - protection failure;
 158: - database, lock, or connection-pool contention;
 159: - accounting/UI discrepancy;
 160: - insufficient evidence.
 161: 
 162: Run Replay only as a controlled comparison with equivalent candles, accepted
 163: prices, strategy version, and settings. State every unavailable input or
 164: material difference. Never claim parity by substituting candle OHLC for missing
 165: price evidence or treating `PENDING` as accepted.
 166: 
 167: ## Required final report
 168: 
 169: 1. **Trade identity and environment** ƒ?" IDs, symbol, version/mode, scope.
 170: 2. **UTC/KSA timeline** ƒ?" evidence-backed sequence and latency breakdown.
 171: 3. **Entry** ƒ?" execution correctness and entry quality.
 172: 4. **Exit** ƒ?" execution correctness and exit quality.
 173: 5. **Stop-loss/profit-lock** ƒ?" worked, failed, or unverifiable, with evidence.
 174: 6. **P&L reconciliation** ƒ?" quantities, prices, fees, and discrepancies.
 175: 7. **Root cause and confidence** ƒ?" confirmed defect, valid loss, configuration
 176:    issue, delay, or insufficient evidence.
 177: 8. **Evidence references** ƒ?" record IDs, query/log timestamps, code/config
 178:    references.
 179: 9. **Missing evidence** ƒ?" exactly what prevents a stronger conclusion.
 180: 10. **Recommended correction and regression checks** ƒ?" only when justified.
 181: 
 182: A profitable trade can violate the rules, and a losing trade can be a correct
 183: execution. State those independently.

===== agent-docs/FINAL_CHANGE_SPECIFICATION_RPR-0.6.md (1-162) =====
   1: # Replay / Production Business-Logic Parity
   2: 
   3: **Revision:** RPR-0.6-DRAFT  
   4: **Status:** NOT APPROVED ƒ?" IMPLEMENTATION FORBIDDEN  
   5: **Baseline:** `0cb6a9a6cc64ba8ac9d33797bcc30af534fd46e3`; the complete dirty-worktree snapshot must be re-hashed and approved immediately before implementation.  
   6: **Supersedes:** RPR-0.4/RPR-0.5 for review only.
   7: 
   8: ## 1. Business objective and flow
   9: 
  10: Replay must use current Production paper-trading policies with an isolated
  11: virtual account. It must never read Production account state, call HTTP, or
  12: call candle-only output historical parity.
  13: 
  14: 1. User selects one symbol, virtual profile, window and mode.
  15: 2. Qualifier validates every input before a generated Replay artifact exists.
  16: 3. A refusal writes only a run/certificate diagnostic.
  17: 4. Qualified analysis uses closed, identity-matching candles.
  18: 5. Simulation invokes pure Production policies with only virtual adapters.
  19: 6. Results stage privately and publish only on terminal success.
  20: 
  21: Release 1 labels outcomes only `ANALYSIS_PARITY`,
  22: `PRODUCTION_LOGIC_SIMULATION`, or `REFUSED_INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE`.
  23: `ENTRY_DECISION_PARITY` and historical wallet-lifecycle parity are out of scope:
  24: they require account-state evidence that Replay is forbidden to read.
  25: 
  26: ## 2. Root cause, evidence and scope
  27: 
  28: Replay currently can include open candles, has null IDs, reads
  29: `wallet_settings`, and differs from Production price/signal management.
  30: Evidence is current source in `RegressionTestWorker`,
  31: `ShadowProductionReplayService`, `ExecutionReplayScope`,
  32: `TradeExecutionValidationService`, `DynamicProfitLockService`,
  33: `LivePositionProtectionService`, `PositionManagementService`, and
  34: `PaperTradingService`.
  35: 
  36: Close-message lineage, deployed JAR/config identity and historical
  37: order-book/derivative/sentiment availability are not durable for all old
  38: windows. Strict claims are therefore unreachable for such eras unless a future
  39: approved forward-capture design is implemented.
  40: 
  41: In scope: single-symbol virtual simulation; qualifier/refusal; closed-candle
  42: identity; virtual settings/constraints; stable Replay IDs; pure policy ports;
  43: staging; resource limits; tests. Out: Production strategy change, real exchange,
  44: Production account reads, multi-symbol scheduling, and retirement of
  45: FIX-11K/FIX-11Q/pre-FIX122/EXPERIMENTAL. Those paths remain explicit
  46: `EXPERIMENTAL` and cannot enter baseline output.
  47: 
  48: ## 3. Read/write boundary
  49: 
  50: | Class | Data |
  51: |---|---|
  52: | Forbidden Production account state | `wallet_asset`, `wallet_trade`, `wallet_managed_position`, `paper_position`, `wallet_settings`, account snapshots and daily account statistics. |
  53: | Permitted read-only evidence | `candle`, `trade_signal`, `market_price_event`, `signal_processing_work`, `candle_analysis_outcome`, `order_book_snapshot`, `fix122_evaluation`, `buy_htf_assessment_audit`, `execution_opportunity`, subject to qualifier as-of rules. |
  54: | Replay-owned writes | `regression_test_run`, `analysis_test_signal`, `wallet_execution_test`, `wallet_position_test`, and new RPR6 profile/certificate/staging objects. |
  55: 
  56: `NON_CANONICAL` and `PROTECTION_NOT_APPLIED` observations are deliberate
  57: exclusions. Required accepted observations are `LEGACY`/`APPLIED`, retaining id
  58: and ordering slot.
  59: 
  60: ## 4. Complete class/file registry
  61: 
  62: | ID | Artifact | Change | Production impact |
  63: |---|---|---|---|
  64: | RPR6-01 | `RegressionTestWorker` | qualifier before staging; closed identity timeline | none |
  65: | RPR6-02 | `ReplayEvidenceQualifier` new | certificate/mode/refusal | none |
  66: | RPR6-03 | profile/store new | immutable virtual account/config | none |
  67: | RPR6-04 | virtual-account adapter new | balances/positions/effects | none |
  68: | RPR6-05 | `ExecutionReplayScope` | stable run keys, clocks, finally cleanup | shared; no non-Replay behavior change |
  69: | RPR6-06 | `ShadowProductionReplayService` | staging, truthful labels, ordered calls | none |
  70: | RPR6-07 | `TradeExecutionValidationService` | Replay settings port | shared; Production regression/latency proof |
  71: | RPR6-08 | `DynamicProfitLockService` | Replay settings port | shared; Production regression/latency proof |
  72: | RPR6-09 | `LivePositionProtectionService`, `PositionManagementService`, `PaperTradingService` | expose pure ordered inputs, no policy reorder | shared; transaction trace/regression |
  73: | RPR6-10 | `PositionExitPolicy`, `ProfitLockPolicy`, `BinanceMinimumExecutionPolicy` | virtual constraints, no Replay HTTP | shared; existing Production provider unchanged |
  74: | RPR6-11 | `OneCandleContinuationGraceReplayObserver` | experimental profile port and label | Replay only |
  75: | RPR6-12 | `RegimeStateService` | guaranteed Replay cleanup | shared; no Production behavior change |
  76: | RPR6-13 | `ReplayParityValidator` | analysis-only oracle | Replay only |
  77: | RPR6-14 | next verified Flyway migration | profile/certificate/staging/trace schema | additive only |
  78: | RPR6-15 | typed replay configuration | explicit modes and resource bounds | runtime protection |
  79: | RPR6-16 | tests | A8 suite | none |
  80: | RPR6-17 | docs | A10 final docs | none |
  81: 
  82: ## 5. Method contract
  83: 
  84: | ID | Area | Required behavior |
  85: |---|---|---|
  86: | M01 | worker start | qualifier replaces the later pending-evidence write guard; it runs before any Replay business write. |
  87: | M02 | timeline/snapshot | require `closed=1` and exact symbol/TF/open/close identity; mismatch refuses before staging. |
  88: | M03 | context | select stored as-of evidence, historical recomputation, or refusal per dependency; never fallback to future data. |
  89: | M04 | staging | no skip semantics. Any mid-run invariant/error discards staged artifacts and records only terminal run failure. |
  90: | M05 | profile port | wallet/account/HTTP access in Replay throws named forbidden-dependency failure before publication. |
  91: | M06 | order | price then signal path: advisory stop, TP without extension, label-free lock, rebase/floor, SELL/add suppression, progressive add. Simulation records price assumptions. |
  92: | M07 | virtual budget | virtual daily-check exception ends `FAILED_PROFILE_EVALUATION`; never consults Production account data. |
  93: | M08 | scope | clear Replay and `RegimeStateService` state in `finally` for success, cancel and error. |
  94: 
  95: ## 6. Database/configuration/logging
  96: 
  97: | ID | Item | Requirement |
  98: |---|---|---|

 100: | D02 | MySQL validation | prove baseline/upgrade; V103 collector dependency must be explicit, never silently satisfied. |

 102: | C02 | `replay.max-concurrent-runs` | `1`. |
 103: | C03 | `replay.profile.required` | `true`; no wallet-settings fallback. |
 104: | C04 | executor/pool limit | bounded; reject before harming Production freshness. |
 105: | L01 | INFO `REPLAY_QUALIFIED` | run/mode/baseline/config/evidence counts. |
 106: | L02 | INFO `REPLAY_REFUSED` | run/requested mode/reason codes. |
 107: | L03 | DEBUG `REPLAY_DECISION` | run/key/route/result/evidence IDs/order/clocks. |
 108: | L04 | WARN `REPLAY_FORBIDDEN_DEPENDENCY` | component/dependency type/run, no secret. |
 109: | L05 | INFO `REPLAY_COMPLETED` | earned mode/rows/duration/resources. |
 110: 
 111: ## 7. Trading behavior
 112: 
 113: Production policy is unchanged. Replayƒ?Ts entry/exit/add/TP/lock/stop behavior
 114: changes only by calling the same extracted pure policies with virtual account
 115: data. Fees/slippage are profile data and zero-fee remains explicitly labelled
 116: when selected. Historical-only/skipped work is reproduced from durable outcome
 117: evidence or analysis parity is refused. Same-boundary higher-timeframe
 118: visibility requires recorded/inferred ordering; otherwise strict analysis claim
 119: is refused.

 128: | X04 | account or HTTP port | fail before publication |

 131: | X07 | virtual budget exception | named failed run, no account read |
 132: | X08 | experimental observer | baseline never invokes; output labelled experimental |
 133: | X09 | cancellation/error | state cleanup and staged artifact discard |
 134: | X10 | concurrent Replay/load | one-run/pool bounds preserve freshness |
 135: | X11 | real MySQL migration | next migration, B105/V103 policy and upgrade path proven |
 136: | X12 | baseline hash | CI rejects mismatched source/config request |
 137: 
 138: ## 9. Risks and rollback
 139: 
 140: Risks: policy drift through shared extraction, latency, look-ahead, Flyway
 141: collision and false labels. Mitigate with pure ports, Production regression and
 142: transaction tests, qualification refusal, preflight and mode metrics. Rollback
 143: disables the new Replay route; it never falls back to wallet reads. Schema is
 144: additive and corrected only forward.

  76: ## Trade investigations
  77: When asked to investigate a trade:
  78: 
  79: - Confirm the application, environment, symbol, and trade ID.
  80: - Use configured database and SSH connections without displaying secrets.
  81: - Use read-only, bounded queries and targeted log searches.
  82: - Read the actual schema before choosing tables or columns.
  83: - Reconstruct the timeline: candle, signal, decision, entry, protection,
  84:   and exit. Display timestamps in KSA (UTC+03:00), interpreting stored
  85:   timestamps as UTC.
  86: - Trace the relevant code and configuration for each decision.
  87: - Check data freshness, entry quality, strategy, risk/reward, fees,
  88:   stop-loss, and exit reason where applicable.
  89: - Assess the entry using only information available at that moment.
  90:   Use subsequent price action separately to evaluate the outcome.
  91: - Distinguish a valid losing trade from a logic defect, stale data,
  92:   execution delay, configuration issue, or missing evidence.
  93: - Check whether the behavior can be reproduced in Replay.
  94: - Support findings with record IDs, log timestamps, and code references.
  95: - State uncertainties; do not invent a cause.
  96: - Do not change code, configuration, services, or production data during
  97:   an investigation unless the user requests a fix.
  98: 
  99: Report:
 100: 1. What happened.
 101: 2. Why the system entered and exited.
 102: 3. Whether the behavior followed the intended rules.
 103: 4. Confirmed defects and missing evidence.
 104: 5. Recommended correction and regression checks, if needed.
 105: 
 106: ## Current architecture direction (pending implementation)
 107: 
 108: The current priority is to remove the trader's runtime dependency on the
 109: collector. This is a design decision, not an implemented/deployed change.
 110: 
 111: - Target runtime: trader-owned Binance kline ingestion and local
 112:   `crypto_ai.candle` writes with `shared-market.mode=OFF`.
 113: - The collector remains independent in `crypto_ai_v2`; the trader must not
 114:   connect to, read from, or write collector stream/cursor tables at runtime.
 115: - Do not run direct ingestion and the shared-market executors together.
 116: - Applied shared-market tables and Flyway history are historical evidence; do
 117:   not drop, reset, or edit them merely to enable the new path.
 118: - `V103__fix_149_fresh_admission_indexes` still requires
 119:   `crypto_ai_v2.market_data_stream_event` for a fresh Flyway install. Treat
 120:   trader-only bootstrap as a release blocker until a forward, tested installer
 121:   policy exists. Never edit an applied Flyway migration.
 122: 
 123: ## Local-candle implementation safety
 124: 
 125: When implementing or reviewing the local-candle design:
 126: 
 127: - Keep `shared-market.fix142.enabled=false`,
 128:   `shared-market.fix142.acknowledgment-enabled=false`, and scheduled analysis
 129:   disabled for the OFF-mode cutover unless a reviewed change explicitly says
 130:   otherwise.
 131: - Preserve the input -> protection -> observer -> close-dispatch transaction
 132:   order. Candle close analysis must not run on the Binance ingestion thread.
 133: - A closed candle needs durable/recoverable analysis admission; an in-memory
 134:   event/queue alone loses work across a crash. Recovery must distinguish fresh
 135:   execution-capable work from historical-only analysis.
 136: - Candle updates must be monotonic: a late open update cannot reopen or
 137:   overwrite a closed candle. Publish close work only for an atomic
 138:   open-to-closed transition.
 139: - A WebSocket reconnect can occur at a candle-close boundary and Binance may
 140:   not replay that final closed-kline event. Candle recovery is automatic: use
 141:   an exact authoritative Binance REST candle only after exchange-time
 142:   finality and the measured live-close grace. It may either finalise a matching
 143:   existing `closed=0` row or insert a wholly absent identity inside the active
 144:   data-quality window. A recovered open row preserves provenance; an inserted
 145:   gap row uses `BACKFILL` provenance. Neither path may alter a completed row,
 146:   create a close-transition token, dispatch analysis, create a live signal,
 147:   or execute a trade. The recovery must have durable retry/audit evidence,
 148:   bounded rate-limit handling, health visibility, and no manual repair step.
 149: - Automation direction: no pending candle or analysis outcome may require
 150:   manual approval, manual database repair, or a persisted trading fence.
 151:   Reconciliation must retry and resolve automatically, or age out to a durable
 152:   explicit outcome with a visible reason. This does not permit unverified data
 153:   to masquerade as valid or force an entry: a genuine strategy/risk veto or
 154:   unavailable authoritative data remains non-executable evidence, not a
 155:   silently stuck pending signal.
 156: - Gap backfill must be a pure insert-if-absent operation. It must never use a
 157:   duplicate-key update that could finalise or alter a live row which appeared
 158:   while the authoritative REST request was in progress. Recovery candidates
 159:   are bounded by the active quality window and terminal audit outcomes are not
 160:   retried. Each candle/work path must write one durable terminal analysis
 161:   outcome; transient failures remain retryable and write no terminal outcome.
 162: - Any future universal trading-mutation barrier is an in-memory drain
 163:   participant only. Outside an active drain it must not block, delay, queue,
 164:   or persistently fence fresh trading, signals, or protection. It may reject
 165:   new mutations only while draining and must then wait for admitted mutations
 166:   to finish before shutdown.
 167: - Historical-only recovery must be structurally unable to reach execution,
 168:   wallet, position, order, or executable-work services. Quarantined work with
 169:   possible financial effects may be concluded automatically only from proven
 170:   durable effect evidence (`EFFECT_COMMITTED` or `EFFECT_ABSENT`); unknown
 171:   atomicity remains a release blocker. Binance REST rate-limit/ban handling
 172:   must be shared by reconciliation, clock sync, bootstrap, and other REST
 173:   callers.
 174: - Enforce freshness from persisted candle close time before analysis and again
 175:   before execution. Historical-only signals must be explicitly marked and
 176:   excluded from live-latency monitoring.
 177: - Price protection must receive the Binance observation timestamp and validate
 178:   age/order inside the protection transaction. Paper-trading effects and their
 179:   accepted price-event state must commit atomically; any future live-exchange
 180:   effect needs a separate idempotent design.
 181: - Do not add manual approval, persisted review fences, or database locks that
 182:   block new analysis, signals, entries, or protection unless the user changes
 183:   that direction. In-process coordination and normal database constraints are
 184:   distinct from persisted trading fences.
 185: - OFF-mode health/admin endpoints must not query or write retired shared-market
 186:   tables. Existing `REVIEW_REQUIRED` history must not block OFF-mode work.
 187: - Keep production and Replay evidence aligned. Replay must fail a parity claim
 188:   for unresolved direct price-event evidence, while deliberate exclusions stay
 189:   excluded.
 190: 
 191: ## Delivery discipline
 192: 
 193: - Treat the local-candle architecture as not deployment-ready until its
 194:   migration/bootstrap policy, concurrency, crash/restart, stale-input,
 195:   Replay-parity, and Java 21/MySQL validation gates pass.
 196: - Do not deploy or change production configuration/data unless the user
 197:   explicitly requests it.
 198: - Protection coverage for open positions during application replacement remains
 199:   an unresolved release gate. Do not represent the local-candle design as
 200:   deployment-ready until it is implemented or conclusively disproven.
 201: 
 202: ## FIX-154: pending higher-timeframe BUY validation
 203: 
 204: FIX-154 is a separate, not-yet-implemented design. Do not merge it into the
 205: local-candle change or describe it as deployed.
 206: 
 207: Purpose: a historical bearish 1h signal may block or delay a current 1m BUY
 208: only when its own evidence is fresh, data-quality-valid, and sufficiently
 209: strong. A historical `btcContextEntryAllowed=false` must remain investigatory
 210: evidence, not a higher-timeframe validity gate; the current 1m signal's own
 211: BTC protection remains unchanged.
 212: 
 213: - Score usable bearish 1h evidence into `NOT_TRUSTED`, `SOFT_CONFLICT`, or
 214:   `HARD_BEARISH_CANDIDATE` using closed-candle local indicators.
 215: - A hard veto requires strong 1h evidence plus fresh, quality-valid bearish
 216:   5m confirmation. Stale, invalid, or unknown historical quality can never
 217:   hard-veto.
 218: - Persist `trade_signal.data_quality_valid` from
 219:   `MarketContextSnapshot.dataValid()` for every new signal. Historical NULL
 220:   means unknown/invalid; never backfill it to true.
 221: - A confirmed 1h recovery may release only a soft conflict and only with fresh,
 222:   quality-valid, non-bearish 5m evidence.
 223: - Preserve the legacy `5M_BEARISH_VETO` behavior: the new data-quality gate
 224:   applies to the new hard-veto/recovery paths, not that legacy fallback.
 225: - Add immutable BUY-evaluation audit evidence; maintain Replay parity and
 226:   prevent future-candle or wall-clock look-ahead.
 227:

 303: 
 304: ## Do Not Start From Zero
 305: 
 306: Existing agreed knowledge should be used as established project context.
 307: 
 308: Agents SHOULD NOT repeatedly rediscover established architecture without
 309: reason.
 310: 
 311: However, persistent documentation is not unquestionable truth.
 312: 
 313: If current code, database evidence, logs, tests, or runtime behavior
 314: contradict persistent knowledge:
 315: 
 316: 1. Report the contradiction.
 317: 2. Do NOT silently accept the old documentation.
 318: 3. Investigate the discrepancy.
 319: 4. Have both agents review the new evidence.
 320: 5. Update shared knowledge only through the dual-agent agreement process.
 321: 
 322: # MANDATORY PRE-IMPLEMENTATION CHANGE CONTRACT
 323: 
 324: This section extends the existing governance. It does not replace any earlier
 325: project, investigation, architecture, trading, safety, or documentation rule.
 326: 
 327: For any material code change, implementation is forbidden until the team has
 328: produced and approved the same exact revision of:
 329: 
 330: `agent-docs/FINAL_CHANGE_SPECIFICATION.md`
 331: 
 332: The specification is the implementation contract and MUST contain, at minimum:
 333: 
 334: 1. Business objective and the exact agreed business line/flow.
 335: 2. Confirmed root cause and evidence.
 336: 3. In-scope and out-of-scope boundaries.
 337: 4. Complete class/file change registry (modify/create/delete/rename/config/
 338:    migration/test/documentation).
 339: 5. Class-by-class and material method-by-method current behavior -> agreed new
 340:    behavior, reason, inputs/outputs, side effects, error handling, concurrency,
 341:    transactionality, Replay impact and Production impact.
 342: 6. Mandatory logging registry covering all materially affected areas. Logs must
 343:    make the business flow and decisions reconstructable without exposing
 344:    credentials/secrets. Include relevant correlation/trade/symbol/interval/
 345:    candle/test identifiers and respect the project's UTC/KSA rules.
 346: 7. Database, configuration and trading-behavior registries, explicitly stating
 347:    NONE when there is no impact.
 348: 8. Test and validation registry, including positive, negative, regression,
 349:    failure/recovery, concurrency where relevant, Replay, and Production-safe
 350:    validation.
 351: 9. Risks, rollback plan, observability plan and documentation registry.
 352: 10. Master implementation registry assigning an ID to every authorized change,
 353:     log, DB/config item, test and documentation update.
 354: 
 355: No source/config/database/trading change may be implemented unless it is
 356: registered in the approved specification. No unrelated refactoring or cleanup
 357: may be hidden inside an approved fix.
 358: 
 359: ## Dual-agent specification approval
 360: 
 361: The SAME exact specification revision must be fully reviewed and explicitly
 362: approved by BOTH agents:
 363: 
 364: - Codex: APPROVED
 365: - Claude: APPROVED
 366: - Unresolved material disagreements: 0
 367: 
 368: There is no majority vote and no fixed review-iteration limit. Any material
 369: revision invalidates both prior approvals and requires a complete re-review by
 370: both agents. Even after dual approval, implementation remains NOT AUTHORIZED
 371: until the user explicitly approves that exact revision.
 372: 
 373: ## Code implementation direction
 374: 
 375: After explicit user approval:
 376: 
 377: - Codex is the implementation owner.
 378: - Claude is the mandatory independent code reviewer.
 379: - Claude reviews the COMPLETE implementation, not only the latest patch.
 380: - Claude comments/rejects; Codex corrects or disputes with technical evidence;
 381:   Claude re-reviews the complete updated implementation.
 382: - Repeat for as many iterations as required. There is no fixed iteration cap.
 383: - Codex cannot self-approve its implementation and the Coordinator cannot
 384:   overrule a material Claude objection.
 385: - Any implementation-code change after Claude approval invalidates that
 386:   approval and requires another complete Claude review.
 387: - A material deviation from the approved specification stops implementation,
 388:   reopens dual-agent design review, creates a new specification revision, and
 389:   requires renewed user approval.
 390: 
 391: ## Post-implementation registry reconciliation
 392: 
 393: Before technical acceptance, reconcile the actual implementation against the
 394: approved master registry. Every planned item must be accounted for and every
 395: actual change must map to an approved registry ID.
 396: 
 397: Acceptance requires at minimum:
 398: 
 399: - missing registered changes: NONE
 400: - unregistered implementation changes: NONE
 401: - unresolved material review findings: 0
 402: - required logging implemented and reviewed
 403: - required tests/validation completed with evidence
 404: - Replay/Production parity reviewed where applicable
 405: - required documentation updates completed
 406: - Claude final full-code review: APPROVED
 407: - Codex final verification of the exact Claude-approved state: PASSED
 408:

 412: facts are candidates for `agent-docs/AGREED_KNOWLEDGE.md`. Candidate text must
 413: be the SAME exact revision reviewed by both Codex and Claude and may be written
 414: there only when BOTH return AGREED. Rejected hypotheses, unresolved disputes,
 415: speculation, and proposed-but-not-implemented architecture must not be stored
 416: as confirmed current state.
 417: 
 418: After an approved implementation is fully reviewed and validated, update the
 419: appropriate Codex and Claude project-memory documents and the shared agreed
 420: knowledge/decisions so future sessions do not start from zero. These updates
 421: must preserve history: supersede old facts/decisions rather than silently
 422: rewriting them.

Show actions, evidence, findings, challenges, disagreements, status changes, and next actions. Do not expose or request private chain-of-thought. When an agent is silent during a long call, a heartbeat may report elapsed time and the declared task only; never manufacture progress.

 483:

If a positive ceiling is reached with material disagreement, status is CONSENSUS NOT ACHIEVED and implementation remains blocked. Continue only through a new/continued formal run with preserved evidence; never manufacture agreement.

 491: 
 492: `scripts/implementation-review.ps1` is the guarded automation entry point for the approved code phase. It MUST refuse to run unless explicit `-UserAuthorized` is supplied for the exact frozen specification. Codex is implementer; Claude is mandatory independent full reviewer. Claude never edits implementation in this loop. Rejection returns to Codex correction, then Claude reviews the complete updated implementation again. A material design deviation stops the loop and requires a revised specification plus renewed dual-agent and user approval. No deployment is authorized by this script.

===== codex-docs/ARCHITECTURE.md (1-285) =====
   1: # Crypto AI Trader Г?" Architecture
   2: 
   3: Status labels used in this document:
   4: 
   5: - **Current** Г?" present in the repository today.
   6: - **Pending** Г?" approved design direction, not implemented or deployed.
   7: - **Legacy/shared** Г?" existing shared-market path retained as historical evidence
   8:   while the trader moves to local-candle ownership.
   9: 
  10: ## Current release status
  11: 
  12: **FIX-153 is implemented in source but is NOT APPROVED FOR PRODUCTION.**
  13: It moves the trader toward collector-free, trader-owned local candle ingestion
  14: in `shared-market.mode=OFF`. Java 21, disposable-MySQL, crash/race, Windows
  15: two-process, soak, OFF-mode and Replay-parity proof gates remain open.
  16: 
  17: FIX-154 is a separate pending higher-timeframe design. It is not implemented
  18: and is not part of FIX-153 deployment scope.
  19: 
  20: ## System purpose
  21: 
  22: The application is a Spring Boot cryptocurrency trader that analyses Binance
  23: market data, persists candles and technical indicators, creates trade signals,
  24: and performs paper-wallet execution and position protection. Production and
  25: Replay are intended to use equivalent persisted market evidence.
  26: 
  27: ## Runtime and data ownership
  28: 
  29: | Area | Current ownership | Target direction |
  30: |---|---|---|
  31: | Trader database | `crypto_ai` | Remains the trader's only runtime database. |
  32: | Collector database | `crypto_ai_v2` | Collector remains separate and is not read/written by the trader at runtime. |
  33: | Candle ingestion | In OFF mode, trader-owned Binance ingestion writes local `crypto_ai.candle`. | Must be proven in a collector-free deployment rehearsal. |
  34: | Candle processing | Bounded lanes, close-transition tokens and a local detector are implemented. | MySQL/race/crash proof remains required. |
  35: | Trading | Paper wallet and managed positions. | Live-exchange orders are outside the local-candle automatic-resolution design. |
  36: 
  37: ## Current primary flow
  38: 
  39: ```text
  40: Binance WebSocket kline
  41:   -> BinanceKlineService
  42:   -> KlineTransactionCoordinator
  43:       -> input transaction: candle + canonical 1m market price event
  44:       -> protection transaction: LivePositionProtectionService
  45:       -> observer transaction
  46:       -> close-dispatch transaction
  47:   -> CandleClosedEvent (AFTER_COMMIT)
  48:   -> CandleAnalysisDispatcher
  49:   -> CandleClosedAnalysisWorker
  50:   -> TechnicalIndicatorService
  51:   -> AnalysisService / TradeSignal
  52:   -> SignalProcessingCoordinator
  53:   -> PaperTradingService / wallet and managed-position state
  54: ```
  55: 
  56: `KlineTransactionCoordinator` preserves input -> protection -> observer ->
  57: close-dispatch ordering. Closed-candle analysis is asynchronous and must not
  58: run on the Binance ingestion thread.
  59: 
  60: ## Current core persistence
  61: 
  62: | Table/domain | Purpose |
  63: |---|---|
  64: | `candle` | Local OHLCV candle history keyed by symbol, interval, and open time. |
  65: | `technical_indicator` | Persisted indicator snapshots derived from closed candles. |
  66: | `trade_signal` | Persisted analysis decisions and their context. |
  67: | `signal_processing_work` | Durable processing registration/status for a persisted signal. |
  68: | `market_price_event` | Canonical 1m price evidence used by protection and Replay. |
  69: | Paper wallet / position tables | Paper executions, open-position state, TP/SL/profit protection. |
  70: 
  71: ## Legacy/shared-market path
  72: 
  73: **Current but pending retirement from the trader runtime.**
  74: 
  75: The shared path includes `SharedMarketSource`, `SharedMarketConsumer`,
  76: `SharedAnalysisDispatcher`, cutover/readiness logic, and local historical
  77: tables such as `shared_market_event_delivery`, `shared_market_consumer_state`,
  78: and `shared_market_health`. These records remain evidence and must not be
  79: dropped, reset, or used to manufacture an approval.
  80: 
  81: The collector event/cursor source is `crypto_ai_v2.market_data_stream_event`
  82: and `market_data_stream_cursor`. In the target local-candle runtime, the
  83: trader must not connect to, read, or write these collector tables.
  84: 
  85: `V103__fix_149_fresh_admission_indexes` is an existing Java Flyway migration
  86: that requires the collector event table for a fresh installation. A trader-only
  87: bootstrap policy is therefore **pending** and remains a release blocker. Do
  88: not edit applied Flyway migrations or Flyway history.
  89: 
  90: ## Implemented, unverified: local-candle architecture (FIX-153)
  91: 
  92: The implemented intended mode is `shared-market.mode=OFF` with trader-owned Binance kline
  93: ingestion and local candle writes. The shared executors and direct-ingestion
  94: path must never run together.
  95: 
  96: Implemented safeguards:
  97: 
  98: - Candle writes are monotonic: a late open update cannot reopen or overwrite a
  99:   closed candle.
 100: - A closed-candle transition is admitted exactly once using an atomic upsert
 101:   result plus in-process admission coordination.
 102: - A bounded local detector recovers a closed candle that was committed before
 103:   the in-memory event/dispatcher could start analysis.
 104: - Fresh candles may execute; stale candles are analysed historically only, or
 105:   their already-owned work is expired without business execution.
 106: - Analysis lanes are bounded. Under overload, 1m may retain only the latest
 107:   pending candle; higher intervals use an explicit bounded FIFO policy.
 108: - Historic shared `REVIEW_REQUIRED` evidence must not block OFF-mode work.
 109: - OFF-mode health/admin endpoints must use local-candle health and must not
 110:   query retired shared-market tables.
 111: - Current REST backfill can finalise an interrupted open row but cannot mint a
 112:   runtime close token or dispatch analysis. Runtime close tokens plus
 113:   provenance keep backfill out of detector admission. The approved, pending
 114:   automatic-reconciliation extension will also insert an exact wholly absent
 115:   candle only inside the active quality window, with `BACKFILL` provenance and
 116:   the same no-token/no-dispatch boundary.
 117: - Observed local behavior (2026-10-09): a WebSocket reconnect at a candle-close
 118:   boundary can leave the already-persisted 1m/5m rows open because Binance does
 119:   not replay the final closed-kline event. The current detector recovers missing
 120:   analysis admission, not missing close facts. An authoritative, bounded REST
 121:   repair may therefore finalise the exact `closed=0` row or, under the approved
 122:   gap-backfill scope, insert an exact absent identity. It must preserve existing
 123:   provenance, use `BACKFILL` for an insertion, create no close-transition token,
 124:   and never dispatch historical work. Automatic reconciliation of this reconnect
 125:   case remains a FIX-153 release gate; the one-time local repair is evidence,
 126:   not proof that the gap is closed.
 127: - Approved pending reconciliation design: gap backfill is insert-only (no
 128:   duplicate-key update); it can never alter a live row that appears during a
 129:   REST request. Candidate selection is bounded to the active quality window;
 130:   terminal audit records are not retried. Every candle/work route must produce
 131:   a durable terminal outcome or remain explicitly retryable after a transient
 132:   failure. Historical-only processing has no execution authority.
 133: - The pending universal mutation barrier is an in-memory drain participant,
 134:   not a database or persistent trading fence. Outside drain it must not delay,
 135:   queue, or block fresh trading, signals, or protection. Open-position
 136:   protection during application replacement remains an unresolved release gate.
 137: - Local source now contains the first reconciliation implementation slice:
 138:   V107 durable audit/outcome tables, update-only authoritative open-row repair,
 139:   pure insert-only gap backfill, a dedicated recovery executor, a reconnect
 140:   request hook, and drain participation. It is disabled by default and remains
 141:   unvalidated on MySQL; it has no deployment approval.
 142: - Direct prices are normalised to `DECIMAL(30,12)` before persistence,
 143:   protection and completion. Equal timestamps have one canonical receipt.
 144: - Every processing attempt is freshness-checked after normal locks using the
 145:   database-to-exchange correction. JVM-origin timestamp decisions use the
 146:   separately measured JVM-to-exchange correction. The measurement performs no
 147:   HTTP under execution locks and uses a bounded five-second time-only client.
 148:   General Binance REST calls use the configured request timeout, and scheduled
 149:   clock sampling has a four-thread Spring scheduler pool.
 150:   Bootstrap obtains time/klines before opening its local candle-write
 151:   transaction, so a stalled REST request does not retain database resources.
 152:   REST bootstrap gets Binance server time before klines.
 153: - An ADMIN-only drain stops analysis admission, exits the JVM on successful
 154:   quiesce, and resumes admission on timeout. Price protection pauses during
 155:   the controlled drain window.
 156: 
 157: ## Implemented, unverified: protection price-event state machine
 158: 
 159: For direct 1m price observations, the target evidence lifecycle is:
 160: 
 161: ```text
 162: PENDING
 163:   -> APPLIED                 (protection effects and state commit together)
 164:   -> NON_CANONICAL           (invalid, stale, out-of-order, or duplicate)
 165:   -> PROTECTION_NOT_APPLIED  (confirmed rollback/crash resolution)
 166: ```
 167: 
 168: Replay and price authority consume only accepted `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` rows.
 169: Replay must reject a parity claim when the requested range contains unresolved
 170: direct `PENDING` evidence; this must not block live trading.
 171: 
 172: The automatic resolver is valid only for the paper-trading phase, where every
 173: protection effect commits in the same transaction as its `APPLIED` update.
 174: Before deployment, the code must prove that there are no asynchronous,
 175: `REQUIRES_NEW`, in-memory-only, or external effects in this boundary. A future
 176: live-exchange order needs a separate idempotent/exchange-reconciliation design.
 177: 
 178: The target runtime also requires exactly one active trader process. This is an
 179: operational prerequisite, not a database lease or fence.
 180: 
 181: ## Pending: FIX-154 higher-timeframe BUY validation
 182: 
 183: FIX-154 is separate from the local-candle change and is not implemented.
 184: 
 185: Its purpose is to let a historical bearish 1h signal affect a current 1m BUY
 186: only when the historical evidence is fresh, quality-valid, and sufficiently
 187: strong. `btcContextEntryAllowed=false` on a historical signal is investigatory
 188: evidence, not a higher-timeframe quality gate; the current 1m BTC protection
 189: remains unchanged.
 190: 
 191: The design includes reliability bands (`NOT_TRUSTED`, `SOFT_CONFLICT`,
 192: `HARD_BEARISH_CANDIDATE`), required fresh/quality-valid bearish 5m support for
 193: a hard veto, persisted `trade_signal.data_quality_valid`, recovery release
 194: rules, immutable outcome audit evidence, and Replay/no-look-ahead parity.
 195: 
 196: The implementation design is maintained in `codex-docs/FIX-154_TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md`.
 197: It supersedes no existing deployed behavior and is deliberately separate from
 198: the unrelated legacy readiness document under `src/main/resources/md/FIX-146.md`.
 199: 
 200: ## Deployment posture
 201: 
 202: Neither FIX-153 nor FIX-154 is deployment-ready. FIX-153 source hardening is
 203: implemented but unverified; FIX-154 remains pending.
 204: 
 205: Required validation includes Java 21 build/tests, disposable MySQL bootstrap,
 206: real-MySQL concurrency and lock-wait tests, crash/restart tests, stale-input
 207: tests, Replay parity checks, OFF-mode health/admin checks, collector-free
 208: runtime verification, deterministic dispatcher/detector/drain tests,
 209: two-process Windows lock rehearsal, and sustained-load observation across
 210: enabled symbols and intervals. This local workspace has Java 17 and cannot
 211: compile the Java 21 project.
 212: 
 213: No production configuration, database, service, or deployment change is made
 214: without explicit user approval.
 215: 
 216: ## FIX-153 validation status (2026-10-09)
 217: 
 218: The reconciliation component now has deterministic Java 21 coverage for its
 219: core admission and outcome contract. The tests execute the real audit and
 220: outcome stores against H2 while mocking only Binance and the candle write
 221: boundary. They prove that healthy delayed live closes are not taken over and
 222: that recovery remains token-free/non-live when it is positively evidenced.
 223: 
 224: The real-MySQL suite contains matching update-only and insert-only assertions,
 225: but it requires a deliberately disposable loopback MySQL instance and is not
 226: run by the ordinary unit-test goal. This is a release gate, not a warning that
 227: can be waived. The shared Binance 429/418 admission gate, universal mutation
 228: barrier, structural historical-only isolation, financial-effect reconciler,
 229: atomicity/crash proofs, replacement-time position protection, Replay parity,
 230: and soak validation remain incomplete. Therefore `local-candle.reconciliation.enabled`
 231: must stay `false`.
 232: 
 233: The single `local-candle` configuration mapping is a startup invariant. It now
 234: contains both reconciliation and existing local-runtime properties; a second
 235: mapping is invalid YAML and prevents the Spring application context from
 236: starting.
 237: 
 238: ### Shared Binance REST admission
 239: 
 240: `BinanceRestAdmissionGate` owns one process-wide REST cooldown. It is consulted
 241: immediately before every call through `BinanceMarketDataClient`; a 429/418
 242: response extends the cooldown using `Retry-After` when it is supplied, otherwise
 243: the bounded fallback. This protects all local REST consumers from repeatedly
 244: calling Binance during a rate-limit/ban response. It is deliberately not part
 245: of the WebSocket or protection path and is not a persisted trading fence.
 246: 
 247: ### Outcome-schema startup invariant
 248: 
 249: The active signal-processing runtime writes `candle_analysis_outcome` for
 250: terminal analysis evidence. V108 therefore ships with the FIX-154 release even
 251: though local-candle reconciliation remains disabled. `CandleAnalysisOutcomeSchemaVerifier`
 252: runs after Flyway and fails application startup with a clear message if the
 253: table is absent; this prevents later processing transactions from failing only
 254: when they attempt their first outcome write.
 255: # FIX-154 implementation update Г?" 2026-10-10
 256: 
 257: `HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentService` is the single pure assessment boundary for
 258: the 5m/1h BUY context. It returns effective decisions separately from the JPA
 259: entities, so a SOFT 1h conflict can be assessed as effective `NEUTRAL` without
 260: ever changing the persisted signal. `TradeExecutionValidationService` uses it
 261: for direct BUY and accumulated-evidence authority; `OpportunityConsolidationService`
 262: uses the same assessment for consolidation. A trusted SOFT bearish 1h limits an
 263: initial entry to 25%; a trusted HARD or unknown bearish 1h rejects it, while
 264: the legacy 5m bearish veto keeps priority.
 265: 
 266: `buy_htf_assessment_audit` is append-only operational evidence. Its writer is
 267: best-effort and uses a bounded dedicated executor: auditing never borrows an
 268: execution transaction's database connection, is skipped in Replay, and cannot
 269: be part of a BUY decision. The progressive add route retains its
 270: existing raw-1h guard; it writes `SOFT_HTF_ADD_BLOCKED` evidence only after
 271: that guard has already rejected an add.
 272: 
 273: `GET /api/fix154/health` exposes non-gating counters for untrusted bearish
 274: blocks, soft caps, soft add blocks, and best-effort audit failures.
 275: 
 276: ## FIX-153 automatic local-candle reconciliation Г?" enabled 2026-10-10
 277: 
 278: In `shared-market.mode=OFF`, `LocalCandleReconciliationService` runs every
 279: five seconds on its dedicated single-worker executor. It finds eligible
 280: missing or interrupted candle identities inside the data-quality window, reads
 281: the authoritative Binance candle, and writes only verified final evidence.
 282: An existing open row is finalized by an update-only path; a missing row uses a
 283: plain insert-if-absent backfill path. Neither route creates a close token,
 284: dispatches analysis, or executes a trade. The next genuine live close resumes
 285: ordinary analysis after the quality window is complete.

  86: # SHIBUSDT #89469 near-TP continuation investigation Г?" 2026-10-10
  87: 
  88: - Status: investigation approved; implementation is explicitly not approved.
  89:   This workstream must not alter or delay FIX-153 release work.
  90: - Hypothesis to test: a breached profit lock close to target may sometimes
  91:   continue when current market structure and momentum remain supportive.
  92: - Important counter-hypothesis: `1h=NEUTRAL` is not positive continuation
  93:   evidence merely because 1m/5m are `WATCH`. Relaxing that guard on distance
  94:   to TP alone could create false holds in weak or sideways markets.
  95: - Required evidence before a policy change: use historical Replay/production
  96:   evidence to compare the current exit rule against a hypothetical bounded
  97:   hold using only information available at the breach. Segment results by
  98:   1h/5m context, regime, trend structure, momentum, retracement depth and
  99:   target-progress band. Evaluate TP hit rate, retained profit, adverse move,
 100:   drawdown, and false-hold frequency. No post-exit price may inform entry or
 101:   exit eligibility.
 102: - Rejected approach: do not authorize continuation from `WATCH/WATCH/NEUTRAL`
 103:   or near-TP distance alone.
 104: - Code-review refinement: `PositionExitPolicy` creates a sharp tier boundary:
 105:   with supportive 5m/current evidence, 1h `WATCH` can hold a breached lock but
 106:   1h `NEUTRAL` falls through to `PROFIT_LOCK_EXIT`. Existing FIX-11T near-TP
 107:   protection arms at 90% target progress and is evaluated after the ordinary
 108:   lock-breach exit, so it could not affect #89469 at 84.28% progress.
 109: - Fee-model refinement: `WalletAutoExecutionService` writes `fee_usdt=0` for
 110:   paper BUY, SELL, and near-TP partial-harvest executions. Any policy Replay
 111:   comparison must add an explicit fee/slippage model before claiming protected
 112:   gross profit is a net gain.
 113: - Approved experiment protocol from independent review: Production policy is
 114:   unchanged. Compare (A) existing immediate lock exit, (B) a one-window,
 115:   near-TP continuation using existing time-safe structure/momentum/context
 116:   calculations, and (C) a volatility-adjusted tighter trailing exit. Use a
 117:   pre-registered 85% TP-progress primary threshold; #89469 at 84.28% does not
 118:   qualify and must not drive threshold selection. Require non-bearish 5m/1h,
 119:   a fee-adjusted protected floor with modeled adverse fills, explicit numeric
 120:   momentum/retracement definitions, no timer resets or repeated extensions,
 121:   and strictly as-of-breach evidence. Evaluate net expectancy, drawdown,
 122:   false-hold/adverse-move rate, and include later-decline counterexamples;
 123:   reserve an out-of-sample period before any implementation decision.
 124: - Final protocol controls: calculate TP progress only from immutable original
 125:   entry and original TP; exclude and report zero/negative/invalid target
 126:   distance. Every A/B/C candidate must use identical historical data,
 127:   evaluation periods, and fee/slippage assumptions. Candidate B must model an
 128:   intrawindow price path sufficiently to determine which of its emergency
 129:   floor or target was reached first; candle high/low alone is not evidence of
 130:   that order. Pre-register the minimum sample size, required net-expectancy
 131:   improvement, and maximum allowed drawdown deterioration before results are
 132:   viewed, then report the qualification count for the B cohort.
 133: - Final cohort and comparison definition: Candidate B is eligible only in the
 134:   85% to strictly-less-than-90% original-TP-progress band, with fresh,
 135:   quality-valid 1h `NEUTRAL`, supportive fresh/quality-valid 5m, and required
 136:   current-frame structure/momentum evidence. Missing, stale, invalid, or
 137:   unknown 1h/5m evidence makes the case ineligible rather than non-bearish.
 138:   This avoids changing or competing with existing FIX-11T behaviour at 90%.
 139:   Pre-count the eligible cohort; insufficient sample means keep collecting.
 140:   B-versus-A is the sole primary comparison; C is secondary/exploratory and
 141:   must have its trailing formula fixed before Replay. Verify ATR, spread, and
 142:   tick-size availability as of breach; if historical spread is unavailable,
 143:   use a declared fixed assumption and sensitivity analysis.
 144: - Final breach semantics: eligibility uses `breach_tp_progress`, calculated
 145:   from the executable price at the exact profit-lock breach, not the historic
 146:   high-water mark. Persist/report both `max_tp_progress` and
 147:   `breach_tp_progress`. The continuation emergency floor is distinct from the
 148:   breached original lock; if actual executable price (including modeled
 149:   fee/spread/slippage) already violates that floor, exit immediately. Freeze
 150:   numerical precision, 90%-boundary precedence (FIX-11T owns 90%+), hold
 151:   length, tolerance, floor, costs, sample threshold, and success criteria
 152:   before inspecting results. Report confidence intervals and chronological
 153:   out-of-sample results; a small qualifying cohort is insufficient evidence.
 154: - Team-2 final validity controls: create and commit a numerical pre-registration
 155:   before any outcome is viewed, recording its immutable content hash here.
 156:   Verify available intrabar path data; where tick/price-event evidence cannot
 157:   order TP versus emergency-floor contact, mark the case ambiguous, apply one
 158:   conservative adverse-move-first rule consistently, and report analyses both
 159:   with and without those cases. Compute the floor per trade as
 160:   `max(original_lock - tolerance, fee_adjusted_breakeven)` and report when
 161:   breakeven binds. Compare A and B as paired outcomes on the same eligible
 162:   breach events, reporting per-event B-minus-A and the count of later declines
 163:   avoided by A.

 165: # Replay-versus-Production parity audit Г?" 2026-10-10
 166: 
 167: - Scope: source-level comparison of the manual Regression/Replay pipeline with
 168:   the current paper-trading Production path. No trading code, configuration,
 169:   database data, or Replay result was changed.
 170: - Confirmed shared authority: Replay regenerates technical indicators through
 171:   `TechnicalIndicatorService`, signals through `AnalysisService`, BUY authority
 172:   through `ExecutionIntelligenceService`, BUY/SELL validation through
 173:   `TradeExecutionValidationService`, and normal exit, TP-continuation,
 174:   profit-lock, and near-TP policies through the corresponding shared policy
 175:   classes. When canonical `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` price events exist, the Replay
 176:   consumes them in timestamp order before making the same close-candle signal
 177:   visible, matching the documented live ordering.
 178: - Confirmed boundary: Replay is a shadow simulation, not a transactionally
 179:   identical copy of Production. It starts with fixed capital of 10,000 USDT,
 180:   manages one symbol/position in isolation, uses replay-local daily budget and
 181:   position state, and writes only `*_test` tables. It therefore cannot
 182:   reproduce an actual wallet balance, simultaneous positions, global
 183:   `maxOpenPositions`, daily loss limit, idempotency races, database locks, or
 184:   real execution latency.
 185: - Confirmed historical-evidence limitation: derivatives history is not
 186:   persisted, so historical Replay always receives `UNAVAILABLE` derivatives
 187:   context when that feature is enabled. Historical order-book evaluation uses
 188:   stored snapshots only and becomes `UNAVAILABLE` when no snapshot exists,
 189:   whereas live Production samples its in-memory/live Binance source. Such runs
 190:   are useful simulations but are not full signal-parity proof unless the
 191:   required historical evidence exists and is reported.
 192: - Confirmed price-evidence limitation: a Replay run labels itself
 193:   `EXACT_PRICE_REPLAY` whenever at least one accepted price event exists. It
 194:   does not prove complete tick coverage for every time an open position needed
 195:   protection. Windows with no accepted events fall back to signal prices and
 196:   are labelled `SIGNAL_PRICE_FALLBACK`; neither fallback nor partial coverage
 197:   should be described as tick-exact reproduction.
 198: - Test evidence: Java 21 focused tests passed for profit-lock/TP-extension
 199:   parity, position exit/continuation, near-TP policy, and shared sizing.
 200:   Existing Replay-specific coverage is narrow: it does not execute a complete
 201:   Production-versus-Replay scenario across entry, add, wallet controls,
 202:   price-event ordering, partial harvest, and exit.
 203: - Conclusion: Replay is suitable for controlled, manual strategy regression
 204:   and the approved FIX-155 research work only when each run carries explicit
 205:   evidence-quality labels. It is not yet valid to claim it is fully identical
 206:   to Production in all phases. A future parity program must add deterministic
 207:   scenario tests and a run-level eligibility contract before using Replay as a
 208:   release-equivalence certificate.

  13: # 2026-10-08 ƒ?" FIX-153 exchange/database clock correction
  14: 
  15: - Problem: JVM/database clock skew could make a Binance-derived candle appear
  16:   too early or too old during freshness checks.
  17: - Solution: `ExchangeClockSynchronizer` measures Binance server time against
  18:   database UTC and JVM time at startup and periodically; database and JVM
  19:   callers apply their own bounded offset without HTTP inside locks.
  20: - The clock-skew warning uses the larger absolute database or JVM offset; the
  21:   dedicated Binance time client has a five-second connect/read bound.
  22: - General Binance REST requests now use the configured request timeout, and
  23:   scheduled clock sampling cannot monopolise the four-thread scheduler pool.
  24: - Bootstrap REST reads occur before its local candle-write transaction, so
  25:   the bounded Binance timeout does not hold a database transaction open.

 110: # 2026-10-09 ƒ?" FIX-153 reconciliation safety correction
 111: 
 112: - Recovery now requires positive loss evidence before it may finalise an open
 113:   row: either the successor candle is already present or a reconnect was
 114:   admitted after the candidate close. This prevents a short healthy-stream
 115:   delay from being converted into a token-free, unanalysed close.
 116: - REST finality/identity disagreement is retryable while the local row remains
 117:   open. The authoritative Binance close timestamp, not the local expected
 118:   value, is persisted after a successful exact match. A bounded first-candle
 119:   probe identifies pre-listing identities as explicit unavailable outcomes.
 120: - Recovery uses the short timeout client and checks drain between requests;
 121:   late-live-close evidence now preserves its first timestamp.
 122: - Status: Java 21 compilation passed. Dedicated reconciliation race, drain,
 123:   MySQL, and Replay tests remain required before enabling the feature.

 127: - Added deterministic Java 21 tests for the reconciliation contract: a healthy
 128:   late live close without successor/reconnect evidence is left untouched;
 129:   successor evidence permits update-only repair and records a non-live outcome;
 130:   a gap-insert race ends as `ALREADY_PRESENT` without inventing an outcome;
 131:   a confirmed exchange gap ends as `SKIPPED_DATA_UNAVAILABLE`; and active drain
